   20: 
   21: H4 is the harness slice that makes such assertions executable: the **oracle capability** on the
   22: shared harness contract, the judge machinery behind it, and the **determinism strategy** so the same
   23: input does not pass one day and fail the next. The item text carries one open spec decision —
   24: "which judge model, and how determinism is achieved — fixed seed, rubric thresholds, or
   25: repeated-vote majority. Decide at the start of this item." That decision is §3 of this plan.
   26: 
   27: ## 2. Honest boundary: no consuming suite exists yet
   28: 
   29: The three consuming ATs live in REQ-009 / REQ-004 / REQ-033 suites, none of which is translated
   30: (just-in-time, per the scope boundary in `loop/bringup/AI4DEV-3-at-harness.md`). REQ-016 — the only
   31: translated suite, and the H7 proving ground — has no semantic AT. The H5 precedent (founder ruling
   32: 2026-08-04) warns that a contract authored without its consuming test is a guess.
   33: 
   34: Why H4 still builds now, and how the guess-risk is contained:
   35: 
   36: - The founder picked this item today; the H7 item text lists H4 among its blockers; the parent
   37:   cannot fold without it. Deferral was available (as it was for the five vendor sims) and was not
   38:   chosen — H4 stayed a numbered slice.
   39: - Unlike a vendor sim (whose contract guesses at an EXTERNAL API's shape), the oracle contract is
   40:   grounded in RATIFIED text: the three AT statements above pin exactly what the oracle must decide.
   41:   We author the three rubrics NOW as committed grounding fixtures, derived line-by-line from the AT
   42:   text, and exercise the full machinery over them in conformance tests. The consuming suites will
   43:   own the final rubric parameters (minimums, tolerances) when they are authored; the rubric SHAPE
   44:   and the judge machinery are what this item pins.
   45: - The surface is kept minimal — one capability, one entry point (`judge`), no speculative modes.
   46: 
   47: ## 3. The open spec decision — RESOLVED (this item, 2026-08-05)
   48: 
   49: ### 3a. "Fixed seed" is REFUTED as unavailable, not merely rejected
   50: 
   51: The Anthropic API exposes **no seed parameter** at all, and on current-generation models
   52: (Opus 4.7+, Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Fable/Mythos) the sampling parameters `temperature` / `top_p` /
   53: `top_k` are **removed — sending any of them is a 400**. Even on older models where temperature
   54: existed, temperature 0 never guaranteed identical outputs. A determinism strategy built on seeding
   55: is therefore not a design option on this provider; the spec's first listed option is closed by
   56: fact, not preference. (Source: claude-api skill, cached 2026-06, checked 2026-08-05.)
   57: 
   58: ### 3b. Judge model: **`claude-opus-5`**, pinned in one module
   59: 
   60: - The judge's job is narrow: binary criterion checks and verbatim extraction over supplied text,
   61:   against a pinned rubric. Reliability of instruction-following under a strict output schema is the
   62:   quality that matters; verdict flip-rate is the enemy, because a flaky gate poisons every suite
   63:   that leans on it.
   64: - Cost is not a deciding factor at this volume: one judge call carries ALL criteria of one rubric;
   65:   3 votes × a handful of semantic ATs × a few KB of material ≈ cents per integration run at
   66:   $5/$25 per MTok.
   67: - `claude-sonnet-5` ($3/$15) is the considered alternative — likely adequate, rejected because the
   68:   saving is immaterial while any increase in verdict variance is paid by every future suite.
   69:   Revisiting later is a one-line change in one module plus re-recording replay fixtures.
   70: - Self-preference risk (the product's Discovery generator is also Claude/Opus) is real for holistic
   71:   "is this good?" judging and largely neutralized here: the judge never ranks or scores freely — it
   72:   answers pinned binary questions against fixture facts supplied in the same prompt.
   73: - `claude-opus-5` is a fixed model id (no date-suffixed snapshots exist to pin). Behavior drift
   74:   under a fixed id therefore CANNOT be excluded by pinning alone — this is a stated reason for the
   75:   replay layer and the provenance record below, which make any drift visible rather than silent.
   76: - Request shape: `output_config: { effort: "low", format: { type: "json_schema", schema: <verdict
   77:   schema> } }`, `max_tokens` sized generously (~4000); thinking left at the model default
   78:   (adaptive). Effort `low` is documented as strong on this model for scoped tasks and reduces
   79:   output variance; the schema-constrained output is the verdict, so there is no prose to drift.
   80: - **No refusal fallback, deliberately.** The claude-api skill recommends server-side `fallbacks`
   81:   by default for product code; for a TEST ORACLE a silent model swap would change the oracle's
   82:   identity mid-suite — the exact "declared fact drifting from a real one" failure this project
   83:   deletes. A `stop_reason: "refusal"` (or `max_tokens`, or unparseable output) is a typed oracle
   84:   ERROR carrying the cause — never a pass, never a silent retry on another model.
   85: 
   86: ### 3c. Determinism: rubric decomposition + repeated-vote majority + committed replay
   87: 
   88: Four layers, each doing the part it is good at:
   89: 
   90: 1. **Rubric thresholds (decomposition).** Every semantic AT is authored as a rubric of NAMED BINARY
   91:    criteria, each judged independently in one structured verdict. Binary questions over supplied
   92:    text at a fixed prompt are far more stable than one holistic judgment. Pass rule v1: every
   93:    `required` criterion true. Thresholds/minimums that the AT itself parameterizes (AT-033.07's
   94:    "configured minimum of N events", "configured tolerance") are rubric PARAMETERS read from
   95:    at-config by the consuming suite — never constants inside a rubric.
   96: 2. **Extraction-then-code-comparison.** Numeric and countable claims are never judged by the model:
   97:    the judge EXTRACTS the stated value or the matched items (verbatim quotes in the verdict), and
   98:    CODE applies the tolerance or the minimum. The model answers semantic questions only
   99:    ("does this answer name the fixture's blocker cause?"); arithmetic is deterministic by
  100:    construction.
  101: 3. **Repeated-vote majority (integration/drill tier).** `judge()` runs k independent live calls and
  102:    takes the per-criterion majority. k = 3, pinned in the at-config registry
  103:    (`harness.oracle.judge_votes`, provisional), so re-tuning is a registry edit, not a test edit.
  104:    For small per-vote flip probability p, the majority flips at ≈ 3p² — the standard variance
  105:    squeeze available on an API without seeds.
  106: 4. **Committed replay (loop tier).** At loop tier the oracle capability is a REPLAY of committed
  107:    recordings — keyed by SHA-256 over (model id, prompt version, rubric id+version+hash, material
  108:    hash, vote index) — no network, no key, bit-deterministic, free. A missing recording is a loud
  109:    typed error naming the key, NEVER a fallthrough to a live call: loop tier stays offline by
  110:    construction. This obeys the tier contract in `registry.ts`: the replay oracle is registered as
  111:    a STAND-IN (legal at loop only); the live judge is registered REAL above loop, so
  112:    `stubbedCapabilities()` stays empty at integration exactly as `openWorld` asserts.
  113: 
  114: **Provenance is part of the verdict.** Every `SemanticVerdict` records model id, `response.model`
  115: as served, prompt version, rubric hash, material hash, vote count, per-criterion vote tallies and
  116: evidence quotes, and source (`live` | `replay`). A drifting judge produces a visibly different
  117: record; a failing verdict is actionable from its own evidence.
  118: 
  119: ## 4. Architecture
  120: 
  121: ### 4a. Contract (types only, in `tests/at/harness/contracts.ts` — ALIASES, never interfaces)
  122: 
  123: ```
  124: Rubric            { id, version, materialSlots: string[], criteria: RubricCriterion[] }
  125: RubricCriterion   { id, kind: 'semantic' | 'extraction', statement, required } —
  126:                   extraction criteria also declare what to extract; code compares
  127: SemanticVerdict   { pass, criteria: CriterionVerdict[], provenance }
  128: CriterionVerdict  { criterionId, pass, evidence, votes: { pass, fail } }
  129: SemanticOracle    { judge(rubric, material: Record<string,string>): Promise<SemanticVerdict> }
  130: AtHarness         gains `oracles: SemanticOracle`
  131: ```
  132: 
  133: Exact member names are the executor's latitude within this shape; the alias rule and the
  134: one-entry-point rule are not.
  135: 
  136: ### 4b. Implementation (`tests/at/harness/oracles.ts`, new)
  137: 
  138: - Rubric validation (refuses empty criteria, duplicate ids, unknown material slot references —
  139:   loud, at construction).
  140: - Versioned prompt builder: system prompt states the judge role, the criteria, and the extraction
  141:   duties; user content carries the material slots. `PROMPT_VERSION` constant participates in the
  142:   replay key — any prompt edit invalidates recordings by construction instead of silently reusing
  143:   them.
  144: - Verdict schema: JSON schema enforced server-side via structured outputs AND re-validated locally
  145:   (a schema drift or refusal must be OUR error, with the cause, never a crash or a pass).
  146:   `stop_reason` checked before parsing; `refusal` / `max_tokens` / unknown criterion ids in the
  147:   response are typed errors.
  148: - Majority aggregation over k votes, per criterion; overall pass = all required criteria pass;
  149:   extraction criteria resolved by code comparison after extraction.
  150: - Replay store: committed JSON recordings under `tests/at/harness/recordings/`; lookup by the key
  151:   in §3c; misses throw with the exact key.
  152: - Live client: official `@anthropic-ai/sdk` (new devDependency — the project is TypeScript and the
  153:   skill mandates the SDK over hand-rolled fetch), constructed lazily; credential read from
  154:   `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` only (see 4d); a missing credential is an error at CALL time with remediation
  155:   text, so building a harness for a suite that never touches oracles stays clean at any tier.
  156: - Transport is injectable for conformance tests (a fake that returns canned responses / throws on
  157:   use), so `at:selftest` never does network.
  158: 
  159: ### 4c. Wiring (`tests/at/harness/index.ts`)
  160: 
  161: `createHarness()` registers the capability by tier: `standInCapability('oracles.judge', replay)`
  162: at loop; `realCapability('oracles.judge', live)` at integration/drill. No change to the
  163: harness-missing message, no change to req-016 behavior (that suite never reaches `h.oracles`).
  164: 
  165: ### 4d. Credential path (`tests/at/harness/runner.ts`)
  166: 
  167: The runner's child environment is an allowlist and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is deliberately leak-tested
  168: OUT of it — that stays true. The judge key uses a DEDICATED name, `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY`, passed
  169: explicitly at the one vitest-spawn call site, ONLY when tier ≠ loop. Loop tier remains
  170: credential-free and offline by construction. `runner.selftest.ts` gains: (a) `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` is
  171: NOT in the base allowlist, (b) the non-loop path passes it through, (c) `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` still
  172: never leaks. `.env.example` documents the variable.
  173: 
  174: ### 4e. Registry pin (`tests/at/harness/atconfig.ts` + `config.ts`)
  175: 
  176: `oracleJudgeVotes` = 3, unit `votes`, `provisional: true`, source: this item's §3c decision;
  177: dotted key `harness.oracle.judge_votes`. (Rubric minimums/tolerances for AT-033.07 are pinned when
  178: the REQ-033 suite is authored — they are that requirement's numbers, not the harness's.)
  179: 
  180: ### 4f. Grounding rubrics (`tests/at/harness/rubrics/`)
  181: 
  182: `at-009-07.ts`, `at-004-10.ts`, `at-033-07.ts` — the three rubrics derived from the ratified AT
  183: text, plus small fixture material samples (a compliant and a violating specimen each), plus
  184: committed recordings so the replay path over real rubric shapes is conformance-tested offline.
  185: Marked as grounding: the consuming suite may amend parameters; the shapes are the contract's proof.
  186: 
  187: ### 4g. Conformance tests (`tests/at/harness/oracles.selftest.ts`)
  188: 
  189: Per suite-authoring rule 2, these are the load-bearing wall — a bug here green-lights every future
  190: semantic AT. Cover at minimum: rubric validation refusals; prompt version participates in replay
  191: key; replay hit (bit-stable verdict), replay miss (typed error naming key, no network attempted);
  192: majority math (3-0, 2-1 both directions, extraction overrides); required-vs-optional pass rule;
  193: refusal / max_tokens / malformed-response / unknown-criterion outcomes are errors, never passes;
  194: loop-tier oracle never touches the network (fake transport that throws if invoked); provenance
  195: recorded; teardown leaves no state.
  196: 
  197: ### 4h. Live smoke (conditional, executor-run, recorded)
  198: 
  199: If `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` is available locally, run the three grounding rubrics once each against
  200: their fixture material via the REAL client (compliant specimen → pass, violating specimen → fail),
  201: capture full verdicts + `response.model` into `loop/items/AI4DEV-20/live-smoke.md`, and refresh
  202: the committed recordings from these real responses. If no key is available, say so in the PR
  203: plainly: the live path ships verified by schema + typed SDK + conformance, and the first
  204: consuming suite's integration run is named as the live proof point. The smoke never runs in CI.
  205: 
  206: ## 5. Steps (executor implements; done-criterion per step)
  207: 
  208: 1. Contract types + `oracles` member → `bun run typecheck` green; type-invention probes still pass.
  209: 2. `oracles.ts` core (validation, prompt, schema, aggregation, replay, live client, injectable
  210:    transport) → its conformance tests green.
  211: 3. Wiring + tier provenance → `at:verify req-016 --tier loop --expect` UNCHANGED (exit 0);
  212:    integration-tier stub assertion unaffected.
  213: 4. Runner credential pass-through + sentinel additions → `runner.selftest.ts` green.
  214: 5. at-config entry + dotted key → config selftests green.
  215: 6. Grounding rubrics + specimens + recordings → oracle conformance green offline.
  216: 7. Live smoke (conditional) → `live-smoke.md` recorded, recordings refreshed.
  217: 8. `bun install` delta (`@anthropic-ai/sdk` devDependency) committed with lockfile.
  218: 
  219: ## 6. Verification state expected at the end
  220: 
  221: | Check | Expected |
  222: |---|---|
  223: | `bun run typecheck` | exit 0 (both projects) |
  224: | `bun run at:selftest` | exit 0, including new oracle conformance |
  225: | `bun run at:verify req-016 --tier loop --expect` | exit 0, byte-identical expectations (no manifest change) |
  226: | CI `verify` on the PR head | green — required for merge, pinned head |
  227: | Live smoke | recorded in item record, or its absence stated in the PR |
  228: 
  229: No AT ids are owned by this item (infrastructure; the parent's done criteria are functional).
  230: Nothing in this item may alter req-016's expected manifest.

  232: ## 7. Risks Gate 1 should attack
  233: 
  234: - The no-consuming-suite boundary (§2): is the grounding-rubric mitigation real or decorative?
  235: - The judge-model ruling (§3b): is `effort: "low"` + schema + k=3 actually the variance-minimizing
  236:   configuration, and is rejecting the refusal-fallback right for an oracle?
  237: - Replay keying: does any input that changes judge behavior escape the key (model id, prompt
  238:   version, rubric, material, vote index — anything missing)?
  239: - Tier semantics: any path by which an integration run silently grades against replay, or a loop
  240:   run silently goes live.
  241: - Credential surface: does `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` pass-through weaken the leak-model the runner
  242:   enforces?
  243: - The `@anthropic-ai/sdk` dependency addition (territory: package.json is neither Lovable's nor
  244:   Claude's per the CI guard; CI code-territory triggers the full suite — is that acceptable?).
  245: - Anything here a tool cannot do as written.

 succeeded in 616ms:
    1: # AI4DEV-3 — AT harness + at-config registry (working spec)
    2: 
    3: **Board:** AI4GOOD-DEV · project W0 Bring-up · vendor-native (closes on merge, no /pm-done gate).
    4: **Role:** the keystone of the verify layer. Every `bun run at:verify` in the skills — the inner
    5: loop's `--tier loop`, `/dev-end`'s assumed-verify, `/pm-done`'s gate — resolves to machinery
    6: that does not exist until this ships. Until then `/pm-done` fails closed by design.
    7: 
    8: This item bundles two things: the **engine that runs acceptance tests**, and the **registry of
    9: every configured number** those tests read.
   10: 
   11: ## Done criteria (functional — this is infrastructure, not itself AT-covered)
   12: 
   13: 1. `bun run at:verify req-0NN --tier <loop|integration|drill>` runs any requirement's suite and
   14:    reports pass/fail PER AT id.
   15: 2. The AT↔code bijection checker passes: every P0 AT id of a requirement has exactly one
   16:    executable test tagged with it; no executable test claims an id that is not in the suite.
   17: 3. The at-config registry is the single source for every configured number; no test hard-codes
   18:    a pinned value.
   19: 4. Proven on one real suite translated end-to-end (proving ground — see scope boundary).
   20: 5. One authored test body demonstrably runs on BOTH surfaces — fixtures and a real screen — from
   21:    a single source. The wiring leaves' `--wired` pass is a RE-RUN of already-assigned ids; if a
   22:    test has to be rewritten to drive a screen, the manifests' "no new AT ids" contract is broken
   23:    and the bijection silently becomes a claim about two different tests sharing a number. No
   24:    product screen exists yet, so prove the seam against the design session's static screens
   25:    (`design/screens/`).
   26: 
   27: ## Part A — the harness (runner + the capabilities the tests assume)
   28: 
   29: 1. **Runner + command surface.** `bun run at:verify req-0NN --tier <tier>`: resolve the executable
   30:    tests for a requirement by AT id, build a clean world, run, tear down, report per id. The
   31:    command shape is fixed — all 30 manifests' done contracts cite it verbatim.
   32: 2. **Tiers — one test, three depths.** `loop` (fast, more stubbed — the inner cycle),
   33:    `integration` (real components + real test DB — the /pm-done gate), `drill` (heaviest —
   34:    fault injection, adversarial). Lets one AT statement serve both the coding loop and the hard
   35:    gate without a rewrite.
   36: 3. **Fixture harness — the "Given" worlds.** Build/tear-down of product-real starting states
   37:    (an NGO; projects across the 9 lifecycle states; ledger rows; blockers; thread messages)
   38:    behind one seam. Reuses/extends the design session's fixture pack. Isolation between tests.
   39: 4. **Controlled clock.** Advance time on demand — funding's 7-day expiry, the UTC daily credit
   40:    reset, blocker aging 48h/7d, abandonment 14d/21d, short-lived link expiry, year-scale
   41:    no-decay. Many tests say "controlled clock" explicitly.
   42: 5. **Sentinels — plant/scan, presence AND absence.** Fact-in-a-file cited in scope (AT-004.16;
   43:    AT-032.12 across all 7 formats); body sentinel that must appear in NO store (AT-009.26);
   44:    key-shaped push that must be BLOCKED (AT-009.29); deleted→recovered file returning its
   45:    original content (AT-032.11).
   46: 6. **Fault injection.** Force a failure at a chosen point: notification atomicity under an
   47:    induced fault between transition and event write (AT-016.09); tamper-evident credit events
   48:    (AT-014.04). Assert rollback / atomicity.
   49: 7. **Semantic oracles.** Judge the MEANING of AI output where string-match cannot: rejection
   50:    copy instructs-never-accuses (AT-009.07); Opus yields a valid scope over 5–10 turns
   51:    (AT-004.10); the assistant's four framed answers (AT-033.07). A judge model or rubric.
   52: 8. **Provider/vendor simulation at the seams.** Controllable stand-ins the tests drive.
   53:    AMENDED (founder ruling, 2026-08-04, on AI4DEV-21's Gate 1 finding): the
   54:    email/notification provider stand-in ships with the H5 item (it has a consuming test,
   55:    AT-016.11); the five named vendor stand-ins — Anthropic per-workspace usage/cost report
   56:    (the whole money path — provider-truth conformance, 20/5/0% stop, nightly
   57:    reconciliation), Stripe (checkout/webhooks/disputes), GitHub, Lovable credit status,
   58:    Linear — are each built with the FIRST test suite that consumes them, never ahead of a
   59:    consumer, because a sim contract authored without its consuming test is a guess that gets
   60:    rewritten when the real suite arrives. Durable owners: board items AI4DEV-38 (Anthropic
   61:    usage/cost sim), AI4DEV-39 (Stripe sim), AI4DEV-40 (GitHub sim), AI4DEV-41 (Lovable
   62:    credit sim), AI4DEV-42 (Linear tree sim), all under this parent so it cannot fold while
   63:    any is missing. Their contracts land in `tests/at/harness/contracts.ts` with conformance
   64:    tests when built (rule 5 below).
   65: 9. **AT↔code bijection checker.** The code-level sibling of `loop/decomp/check-tree.ps1`:
   66:    every P0 id has a tagged executable test, no invented ids. Prevents silent skips.
   67: 10. **Per-id reporting** consumable by the skills (green/red per AT id + summary).
   68: 
   69: ## Part B — the at-config registry (single source of pinned numbers)
   70: 
   71: One place holding every configured value; tests read it, never hard-code. Contents (initial):
   72: gateway latency p95 ≤300ms + first-chunk ≤500ms (PROVISIONAL — pending founder SLO); AUP
   73: residual removal ≤15min; monitor propagation ≤5min; $50 funding minimum; $200 first-fund cap +
   74: per-day cap; tier grants 10/30; blocker aging 48h/7d; abandonment 14d/21d; 4-hour restore
   75: objective; file per-file + per-project size caps; authorized-link TTL; OD-4 binding-check
   76: threshold; OD-7 PRD-gate threshold. One edit re-tunes every test that uses a value — the reason
   77: harness + registry ship together.
   78: 
   79: ## Three execution surfaces (matches the manifests' surface tags)
   80: 
   81: - **backend** — against edge functions + the test DB.
   82: - **ui** — the wired re-runs, driving real screens. NOTE: the design session PLANNED a
   83:   design-check Playwright harness at `tests/design/` but never built it — only the static
   84:   screens, change orders, gate reports and log exist. There is no UI test foundation to inherit;
   85:   this item builds it. (The design checks themselves — never-show list, units discipline — are a
   86:   standing suite over the screen set, NOT per-requirement AT work; out of scope here.)
   87: - **skill** — REQ-028's tests in a harness-driven Claude Code session against fixture repos.
   88: 
   89: **Surface is an axis independent of tier.** A test is authored once and selected by id; the
   90: harness decides which surface it drives. 22 of 30 requirements are `mixed` and 21 carry a wiring
   91: leaf, so this is the common case, not an edge case.
   92: 
   93: ## Decisions that must land BEFORE the first suite is translated
   94: 
   95: These three are conventions, not code — none of them needs the harness to exist. All three are
   96: cheap now and expensive later: every suite translated before they are settled has to be rewritten.
   97: 
   98: 1. **The surface seam + the authoring rule.** A test that will be re-run through a screen must
   99:    never poke a fixture directly; it asserts through a seam the harness points at either the
  100:    fixture world or a rendered screen. Write the rule down where test authors will meet it
  101:    (`/dev-start`'s brief), because the just-in-time authoring moment for a UI-relevant test is
  102:    its OWNING leaf — early — while the wired re-run happens at the wiring leaf, late. An author
  103:    who does not anticipate the second run produces a test that cannot have one. RESOLVED
  104:    (founder, 2026-07-28): tests interact with the system ONLY through a driver interface
  105:    supplied by the harness; the harness provides a fixture driver now and a screen driver
  106:    later; a test that imports or touches fixture internals directly is invalid.
  107: 2. **A stable-identifier convention for screen elements.** Lovable owns the interface and
  108:    regenerates markup on every message; a wired test pinned to whatever markup existed that day
  109:    breaks on the next Lovable turn. Nothing in `design/ui-ux-instructions.md` mandates test
  110:    handles today. Agree the convention and push it into Lovable's project knowledge (the channel
  111:    already carrying the governance rules) so it re-applies to every generation. DRAFTED:
  112:    `loop/bringup/testid-convention-draft.md` — awaiting founder ratification.
  113: 3. **Where the ui tag lives.** 21 manifests scope `--wired` to "the ui-tagged subset"; no
  114:    acceptance file carries such a tag — the subset is named in 21 places and defined in zero.
  115:    Decide: marked per test in `acceptance/at-req-0NN.md`, or declared per leaf in the manifest.
  116:    Until this lands, `--wired` has no defined input. RESOLVED (founder, 2026-07-28): the ui tag
  117:    lives in the executable test registration — `atTest` accepts a surface marker; the acceptance
  118:    markdown files are NOT edited; `--wired` selects registered ui-marked ids and fails the run if
  119:    a wiring leaf's selection is empty.
  120: 
  121: ## Suite-authoring rules (founder-ratified 2026-07-29, after the REQ-016 audit)
  122: 
  123: Origin: the founder suspected the translated REQ-016 suite was overbuilt; an audit plus one
  124: codex round (gpt-5.6-sol, xhigh) confirmed the WASTE IS IN THE TRANSLATION PATTERN, NOT THE
  125: PLAN — the ~658 P0 ids stay; the acceptance docs are NOT reopened. These rules bind every suite
  126: translated from here on. The REQ-016 suite predates them and is retrofitted when H2 lands.
  127: Verdict trail: two of the auditor's own cuts were REFUTED and are recorded here as
  128: must-keeps, so nobody re-proposes them.
  129: 
  130: 1. **Capture once, assert many.** For each event execution, capture raw events, deliveries and
  131:    linked outcomes under the returned eventId, then FREEZE that snapshot (immutable — one lens
  132:    that normalizes or de-duplicates in place hides evidence from the next). Every P0 id whose
  133:    assertions only PROJECT that evidence (recipients, channels, payload, body, ops outcomes)
  134:    consumes the snapshot without re-firing. In REQ-016 this makes ~11 integration-tier firings
  135:    free: AT-016.04/.05/.12 become lenses over AT-016.03's capture.
  136: 2. **Generic self-checks live in the harness, once — with their own conformance tests.** Sentinel
  137:    value quality (length/uniqueness), rejection of unknown fault points, failure on clearing a
  138:    never-fired fault, restart epoch change, controlled-clock product wiring: implemented and
  139:    independently tested in the harness, never re-asserted per suite. HARD CAVEAT (codex): a bug
  140:    in a centralized guard green-lights all 30 suites at once — e.g. a fault handle that counts
  141:    "armed" as "triggered" makes every atomicity test pass on nothing. The conformance tests are
  142:    therefore not optional polish; they are the load-bearing wall. Domain-SPECIFIC controls stay
  143:    in the suites: no-fault control runs, mid-flight-not-yet-delivered checks, meaningful-config
  144:    guards, role-actually-moved checks — those are scenario evidence, not boilerplate.
  145: 3. **Fresh world only when state demands it.** A new open() only when the scenario changes
  146:    fault/provider/clock/process/role state, or when zero work could satisfy the assertion (then
  147:    include a positive control). Register each P0 id exactly once; loop its full oracle matrix
  148:    INSIDE the test — never sample, never mint per-row tests.
  149: 4. **Must-keeps (cuts proposed and REFUTED — do not re-propose):** (a) the sole-writer proof
  150:    keeps its domain firings — the provider-side orphan check is vacuous when nothing fires, and
  151:    a static import scan cannot see a sender using a raw client; the firings ARE the evidence.
  152:    (b) Oracle logic (channel rules, payload predicates, pair counting) and harness primitives
  153:    get focused unit tests of their own — they are code nothing else checks, and per rule 2 a
  154:    silent oracle bug would invalidate every suite. The tiers do not substitute for this.
  155: 5. **Shared contract, thin adapters.** Tier/clock/sentinel/fault/config/vendor contracts come
  156:    from ONE shared harness package (with the conformance tests above); a suite defines locally
  157:    only its requirement-specific SUT and fixture adapter. `_contract.ts` in REQ-016 was the
  158:    pre-harness stopgap, not the pattern.
  159: 
  160: ## Dependencies
  161: 
  162: - Integration tier needs a real test database → the **staging Supabase item (AI4DEV-6)** is a
  163:   sibling it leans on.
  164: - No downstream can be verified until this exists; it is intentionally first in W0.
  165: 
  166: ## Scope boundary — what this does NOT include
  167: 
  168: It builds the ENGINE + capabilities, not the ~658 per-requirement tests. Those are translated
  169: **just-in-time, test-first, when each requirement is pulled** (`/dev-start`'s brief points at
  170: the leaf's verify set). This item ships **one real suite translated end-to-end** as its proving
  171: ground — **REQ-016 (Notifications)** recommended: 12 P0, small, and it exercises the emitter,
  172: atomicity/fault-injection, and provider-acceptance paths in one suite.
  173: 
  174: ## Suggested internal breakdown (for when this item is pulled)
  175: 
  176: - **H0 Conventions** — the three decisions above, settled and written down. No code; blocks H7
  177:   and every just-in-time translation that follows.
  178: - **H1 Runner + tiers + surfaces + reporting** — the `at:verify` command, tier dispatch, the
  179:   surface seam and `--wired` selection, per-id report, the bijection checker.
  180: - **H2 Fixture harness + controlled clock** — world build/teardown seam + time control.
  181: - **H3 Sentinels + fault injection** — plant/scan utilities + induced-fault hooks.
  182: - **H4 Semantic-oracle harness** — the judge/rubric surface.
  183: - **H5 Vendor sims** — the email/notification provider stand-in now; Anthropic usage/cost,
  184:   Stripe, GitHub, Lovable, Linear stand-ins deferred to their first consuming suites
  185:   (AI4DEV-38..42 — see Part A item 8's amendment, founder 2026-08-04).
  186: - **H6 at-config registry** — the pinned-value module every test reads.
  187: - **H7 Proving ground** — translate REQ-016's 12 P0 end-to-end at integration tier; green. Its
  188:   in-app surface also exercises done-criterion 5 (one authored body, both surfaces).
  189: 
  190: ## Open questions for the build
  191: 
  192: - Test framework (Vitest/Playwright split for backend vs ui) — decide at H1. Constrained by the
  193:   surface seam: whatever is chosen, one authored test must reach both surfaces.
  194: - Semantic-oracle model + determinism strategy (fixed seed / rubric thresholds) — H4.
  195: - How much the `loop` tier stubs vs `integration` — the stub boundary — H1/H2.
  196: - Provisional pins (gateway p95, OD-4, OD-7) stay flagged in the registry until founder-set.

   19: 
   20: - **AT-033.06 (P0)** — Given the assistant's capabilities, When mutation is attempted through it by any phrasing (four probes: set a requirement status, resolve a blocker, accept a scope addition, move money/top-up), Then no mutation path exists — the underlying requirement, blocker, ledger, and scope records are unchanged (sentinels) — the assistant is strictly read-only.
   21: - **AT-033.07 (P0)** — Given controlled fixtures (a named in-progress task, an open blocker with a known cause and a known awaited action, a recent-activity set of N known events, and a known fuel balance + burn rate whose runway is a defined value: balance ÷ burn), When the NGO asks the four framed questions, Then each answer passes its defined oracle: the STATUS answer states the fixture task as current work; the blocker answer names the fixture cause AND the awaited action; the progress answer includes at least a configured minimum of the N fixture events and introduces NO fabricated facts; and the runway answer states the defined runway value within a configured tolerance. [cx] [cx r2: each oracle pinned — facts, minimums, formula, tolerance] [cross: AT-026.02 owns the mirror's write-path absence]
   22: - **AT-033.08 (P0)** — Given a SCOPE ask and separately a PRIORITY ask (two probes), When the assistant responds to each, Then each response passes the REQ-025 protocol checklist: the in-thread volunteer acceptance AND the volunteer-created linked task are both named as required before work; the assistant MAY pre-fill a draft — and the submission act remains the NGO's own: no assistant-originated thread message or task exists without the NGO submitting. [cx r2: both branches parameterized; the explanation judged against the full protocol (acceptance + linked task), not acceptance alone] [cross: REQ-025/AT-025.01/02]
   23: 
   24: ## D. Surface reuse & control posture
   25: 
   26: - **AT-033.09 (P0)** — Given the `matched_pending_fuel` → `in_progress` KICKOFF transition (the reframe trigger — not funding), When the project page chat is compared before and after, Then the EXISTING chat surface/infrastructure is reused (the same chat surface, not a newly introduced one) and the model identity is the same as Discovery's — while a separate conversation record and an assistant-specific prompt/configuration are PERMITTED (the read-only reframe requires them); a text question gets a text answer (on-demand Q&A exercised), and no assistant message is ever initiated without an NGO request. [cx: trigger corrected to kickoff] [cx r2: same-component/route/conversation-id invariants over-specified — reuse + model identity is what the requirement states]
   27: - **AT-033.10 (P0)** — Given fuel provisioned to cover the FULL fixture sequence (a defined finite turn count, staying clear of the zero-fuel stop and provider rate limits), When the NGO submits the named harmless off-project prompts from the fixture list plus the defined repeat-turn sequence, Then NO response is declined, redirected, capped, or gated FOR PROJECT-SCOPE RELEVANCE — the absent guardrail is the SCOPE guardrail; the cost display is present on every turn (the control); the zero-fuel stop (AT-033.05), budget commitments, and AUP controls remain untouched and out of this test's scope. [cx] [cx r2: fixtures pinned — named prompts, finite count, fuel sized to the sequence, rejection-reason scoped]

    1: # AT-REQ-004 — AI Discovery Agent (free, rate-limited)
    2: 
    3: Source: prd-mvp.md REQ-004 (isolated: requirements/req-004.md). Dependencies: REQ-003.
    4: 
    5: **Boundary note [cx]:** REQ-004 owns Discovery behavior, credits/routing at the Discovery boundary, tiers, fit-check/declines, guardrails, and structured output. Tested in their owning suites (setup/cross only here): the **auto `discovery_in_progress → scoped`** transition and the **invalid-output → bounded-retry → admin** rule → AT-REQ-005.5; **funding mechanics** ($50 min, NGO-picked amount, reactive top-up) and the **Discovery ledger label** → AT-REQ-006; **REQ-034 category attribution** → AT-REQ-034; the **UTC reset boundary** → AT-REQ-002.
    6: 
    7: ## A. Credits & the two-layer money model
    8: 
    9: - **AT-004.01 (P0)** — Given an unverified-tier NGO, When it converses with Discovery, Then consumption draws from the 10/day allowance; Given a vetted NGO, Then from 30/day [cross: REQ-002].
   10: - **AT-004.02 (P0)** — Given free-credit turns with controlled provider costs, When each completes, Then the credit charged holds a constant cost-to-credit ratio (proportional to platform cost, under a stated rounding rule), and each turn's cost is shown to the NGO. [cx r2: proportionality, not just monotonicity]
   11: - **AT-004.03a (P0)** — Given an UNVERIFIED-tier NGO at 0 credits on an unfunded project, When it sends a message, Then it is blocked and shown exactly: get vetted (→30), fund fuel now, or wait for the next day. [cx: split by tier]
   12: - **AT-004.03b (P0)** — Given a VETTED NGO at 0 credits on an unfunded project, When it sends a message, Then it is blocked and shown only the applicable remedies: fund fuel now, or wait — never "get vetted". [cx: split by tier — REQ-002]
   13: - **AT-004.49 (P0)** — Given free-phase Discovery over a day, When turns run against the allowance, Then total free-phase provider spend never exceeds the remaining allowance and the balance never goes negative — without prescribing whether an underfunded turn is preflight-rejected or hard-capped. [cx r2: bound platform spend, do not mandate "does not run"]
   14: - **AT-004.04 (P0)** — Given a funded project (fuel balance > 0), When the NGO runs Discovery on it, Then every turn bills the project's fuel — never the free pool ("Funded → all-$"), and the same platform share applies as any consumption [cross: REQ-006].
   15: - **AT-004.05 (P0)** — Given one NGO with a funded project A and an unfunded project B, When it runs Discovery on both, Then A's turns bill A's fuel and B's turns draw the NGO's free daily pool — the pool serves only unfunded projects.
   16: - **AT-004.47 (P0)** — Given one NGO with two UNFUNDED projects, When it runs Discovery across both, Then their combined free consumption cannot exceed the NGO's single daily grant — the allowance is per-NGO, not per-project. [cx: added]
   17: - **AT-004.48 (P0)** — Given a funded project with exhausted fuel AND the NGO holding a positive free-credit balance, When Discovery runs on that project, Then it does NOT fall back to free credits — it requires a fuel top-up. [cx: added — funded projects never draw the pool]
   18: - **AT-004.06 (P0)** — Given an NGO that funds a project mid-conversation, When the next turn runs, Then billing switches to fuel from that turn onward.
   19: - **AT-004.07 [retired — cx r2: duplicate of AT-004.04, which already asserts funded turns bill project fuel at the standard share]**
   20: - **AT-004.08 (P0)** — Given free credits, When a day rolls over, Then the balance hard-resets to the tier grant with no rollover [cross: REQ-002 owns the exact UTC boundary]. [cx]
   21: - **AT-004.09 (P0)** — Given funding, When it occurs, Then the model ID and service tier / request-priority settings are identical before and after, and the free daily allowance is unchanged — funding removes only the wait. [cx r2: observable request settings instead of nondeterministic "latency class"]

   23: ## B. Conversation behavior
   24: 
   25: - **AT-004.10 (P0)** — Given a representative non-technical intake fixture, When Discovery runs to completion on Claude Opus over 5–10 structured turns, Then its output satisfies a fixture-specific semantic oracle — the required facts, constraints, user stories, and acceptance criteria for that fixture are present and correct — not merely non-empty fields. [cx r2: 5–10 bound + semantic oracle, not field-presence]
   26: - **AT-004.11 (P0)** — Given a Discovery conversation in progress, When the NGO leaves and returns (new session, next day), Then the conversation persists and resumes with full prior context.
   27: - **AT-004.12 (P0)** — Given free-credit Discovery, When the NGO asks an unrelated task (general Q&A, document drafting, translation, coding help), Then the agent declines/redirects to scoping.
   28: - **AT-004.13 (P0)** — Given repeated off-topic requests on free credits, When the pattern continues, Then a plain notice is shown and the conversation is flagged for founder visibility — and the NGO is never locked out.
   29: - **AT-004.14 (P0)** — Given a free conversation reaching the bounded per-conversation turn ceiling, When the ceiling is passed, Then Discovery wraps up: it generates the scope or directs the NGO to start a fresh Discovery.
   30: - **AT-004.15 (P0)** — Given a funded project's Discovery, When the NGO goes off-topic AND continues past the free turn ceiling AND repeats off-topic requests, Then no free-scope redirect, turn-ceiling wrap-up, notice, or founder flag occurs — only the per-turn cost display and fuel gauge apply. [cx r2: exercises the full "funded = no guardrail" clause, not one off-topic turn]

[cx: sentinel makes citation observable, not "may cite"]
   35: - **AT-004.17 (P0)** — Given a file NOT marked Discovery-visible, When the conversation runs, Then that file's content never reaches the agent (negative test at the context boundary).
   36: - **AT-004.18 (P0)** — Given a mid-conversation need, When the agent asks for more material, Then the NGO can upload and the agent uses it in later turns.
   37: - **AT-004.19 (P0)** — Given a file attachment on free credits, When uploaded, Then it consumes zero credits and triggers no confirmation interruption; Given a funded project, Then file-bearing turns bill fuel like any turn.

[d90: cause labels added to the enumerated contract — see AT-004.58-60 for generation behavior]
   42: - **AT-004.21 (P0)** — Given any Discovery output or rendered scope doc, When inspected, Then no project/build-cost estimate appears and the complexity tier is never expressed in money — the mandated ~$25/mo Lovable maintenance figure and per-turn costs are permitted. [cx: narrowed — was "no dollar anywhere", which contradicted the required maintenance figure]
   43: - **AT-004.22 (P0)** — Given every generated scope, When inspected, Then both parts of the build split are present (which parts are built in Lovable and which are coded through Claude Code) — v1 always emits both.
   44: - **AT-004.23 [retired — cx: the `discovery_in_progress → scoped` auto-transition is a REQ-005.5 lifecycle obligation → AT-REQ-005.5]**
   45: - **AT-004.24 (P0)** — Given a scoped project, When the INITIAL automated build backlog is decomposed [cross: REQ-026/036], Then it derives from the passing dev-authored PRD and no task decomposes Discovery output directly (later volunteer-added sub-issues / accepted scope-additions are exempt). [cx r2: scoped to initial decomposition — later sub-issues are allowed by REQ-026]
   46: - **AT-004.52 (P0)** — Given a completed Discovery, When PRD authoring and the completion scorer run [cross: REQ-036], Then the Discovery scope is the source supplied to PRD authoring AND the reference the scorer compares the PRD against. [cx r2: covers "scope contract = PRD source + scorer gate reference", not just downstream lineage]
   47: - **AT-004.25 (P0)** — Given rendered scope docs across Tier 0, Tier 1, and Tier 2 fixtures, When read by the NGO, Then each plainly explains its assigned data tier (Tier-2 additionally renders fixtures-only handling), the complexity tier with rationale + start-small advice, maintenance expectations (chat-evolve, ~$25/mo paid directly, NGO owns the code), and links Lovable pricing where recommended. [cx r2: parameterized across all tiers, not Tier-2 only]
   48: - **AT-004.58 (P0)** — Given a shared vocabulary already containing a cause label (fixture: "food security") and a new Discovery conversation whose problem description clearly matches that same domain, When the scope generates, Then the emitted cause label REUSES the existing "food security" label rather than inventing a synonymous new one (e.g. "hunger relief" or "food banks") — generation normalizes against the existing vocabulary, it does not invent freely per project. [d90]
   49: - **AT-004.59 (P0)** — Given a Discovery conversation describing a domain with no matching existing label, When the scope generates, Then a new cause label is added to the shared vocabulary — the vocabulary grows only for genuinely new domains; and Given a conversation too thin to support a confident label, When the scope generates, Then zero cause labels are emitted — generation may legitimately produce none, causes are never a required output. [d90]
   50: - **AT-004.60 (P0)** — Given a scoped project carrying Discovery-generated cause labels, When the NGO removes one, Then it is removed; When the NGO or any other account attempts to type, create, or curate a cause label through any supported UI/API, Then no such control or endpoint exists anywhere in the product — there is no admin taxonomy-management surface either. Correction is deletion-only; invention stays machine-owned. [d90]

   52: ## E. Data-sensitivity tiers
   53: 
   54: - **AT-004.26 (P0)** — Given any Discovery, When the conversation proceeds, Then the agent asks what data the tool will handle BEFORE assigning a sensitivity tier.
   55: - **AT-004.27 (P0)** — Given ordinary-PII needs, When tiered, Then Tier 1 is assigned with a minimization reminder and the NGO data-responsibility acknowledgment.
   56: - **AT-004.28 (P0)** — Given special-category or high-volume PII, When tiered, Then Tier 2 is assigned: synthetic/anonymized fixtures only during build; the NGO connects real data itself after completion; real Tier-2 data never reaches Anthropic, Lovable, or the volunteer.
   57: - **AT-004.29 (P0)** — Given health, immigration, abuse-victim, or financial data, When tiered, Then the tier is never below Tier 2.
   58: - **AT-004.30 (P0)** — Given genuine uncertainty about the data, When tiered, Then Tier 2 is assigned (unsure → Tier 2).
   59: - **AT-004.31 (P0)** — Given a no-restriction tool (no personal data), When tiered, Then Tier 0 is assigned. [cx: promoted P1→P0]
   60: - **AT-004.50 (P0)** — Given a tier assignment, When it lands, Then the data-exposure responsibility is attributed to the NGO, and a data-responsibility acknowledgment is required in the tier flows that mandate one (Tier 1 and Tier 2) — not universally for Tier 0. [cx r2: acknowledgment only where the tier rules require it]
   61: - **AT-004.51 (P0)** — Given a Discovery-assigned data tier — including one where post-Discovery scope EDITS changed the data surface (second fixture), When the project reaches triage [cross: REQ-023], Then the tier and the final edited scope are presented to the HUMAN reviewer with the advisory pass's data-tier evidence, and the tier is confirmed by the reviewer's decision — never treated as final without human confirmation. [cx: added] [d74: confirmation is the reviewer's, informed by advisory evidence; post-edit drift covered]
   62: 
   63: ## F. Maintainability-fit check & declines
   64: 
   65: - **AT-004.32 (P0)** — Given a need a non-technical staffer can maintain via Lovable chat (internal tools: intake forms, CRUD trackers, directories, dashboards), When checked, Then it passes the fit check (fit by default).
   66: - **AT-004.33 (P0)** — Given a need requiring ongoing developer maintenance (developer-grade, one-off, pure-backend, or Tier-2 data that cannot live in Lovable), When checked, Then Discovery declines plainly, explains the limitation, and produces NO publishable scope.
   67: - **AT-004.34 (P0)** — Given a confidential-codebase need, When checked, Then it is likewise declined (public-only v1).
   68: - **AT-004.35 (P0)** — Given any decline, When it lands, Then a decline record exists for founder review — and there is no waitlist and no second track.
   69: - **AT-004.36 (P0)** — Given a tool handling sensitive *data* that otherwise fits, When checked, Then it is not declined for sensitivity — it is served, with the tier assigned by the 0/1/2 rules (ordinary PII → Tier 1, special-category → Tier 2). [cx: corrected — not everything sensitive is Tier-2]
   70: - **AT-004.53 (P0)** — Given a fit decline of either cause (ongoing developer maintenance; confidential codebase), When it lands, Then ALL of the following exist from that one event: the NGO's decline message; a durable decline record ON THE PROJECT carrying cause, date and reshaping suggestion; exactly ONE platform-admin ops item carrying the project, the cause and the full Discovery conversation; and the admin notification (REQ-016). A decline that produces the message but no ops item, or two ops items, fails. [d89]
   71: - **AT-004.54 (P0)** — Given a declined project, When the NGO views its project list, Then the declined project is still listed with its decline record visible — the decline is not chat-only. And Given the decline message itself, Then its copy states that a person reviews every decline and will reach out if it was wrong (the oversight sentence is present, not implied). [d89]
   72: - **AT-004.55 (P0)** — Given an open fit-decline ops item, When the founder disposes it **upheld**, Then the item closes, the disposition is recorded with actor and timestamp, the project stays `cancelled`, and the NGO receives NO further notification. [d89]
   73: - **AT-004.56 (P0)** — Given an open fit-decline ops item, When the founder disposes it **overturned**, Then the project returns to `discovery_in_progress` with its Discovery conversation intact and resumable, the NGO is notified that Discovery reopened, and the disposition is recorded. [d89] [cross: REQ-005.5 owns the transition legality]
   74: - **AT-004.57 (P0)** — Given a set of disposed fit-declines, When the calibration dataset is read, Then every disposition (upheld and overturned) is retrievable with its decline cause and Discovery conversation — the dataset the decline evaluator is tuned against, parallel to REQ-023's founder-review records. An unauditable decline — one with no ops item or no recorded disposition path — fails. [d89]
   75: 
   76: ## G. Regeneration, retries & failure
   77: 
   78: - **AT-004.37 (P0)** — Given a generated scope the NGO rejects, When they regenerate, Then regeneration works a bounded number of times, each with a logged reason, and costs zero credits.
   79: - **AT-004.38 (P0)** — Given the regeneration bound is exhausted, When the NGO tries again, Then the case escalates to an admin instead of regenerating.
   80: - **AT-004.39 (P0)** — Given a system-error mid-turn, When the turn is retried, Then the retry costs zero credits.
   81: - **AT-004.40 [retired — cx: invalid-output → bounded-retry → admin (and not silently reaching `scoped`) is a REQ-005.5 lifecycle obligation → AT-REQ-005.5]**
   82: 
   83: ## H. Abuse guardrails & controls
   84: 
   85: - **AT-004.41 (P0)** — Given an email-unverified account, When it attempts any Discovery message, Then it is blocked [cross: REQ-001/002].
   86: - **AT-004.42 (P0)** — Given a per-NGO admin kill switch, When an admin disables the NGO, Then its Discovery is blocked immediately.
   87: - **AT-004.43 (P0)** — Given free credits, When any admin path attempts to grant supplemental free credits, Then no such capability exists.
   88: - **AT-004.44 (P0)** — Given heavy usage by one NGO, When its caps bind, Then only that NGO is limited — there is no platform-wide Discovery circuit breaker.
   89: - **AT-004.45 (P0)** — Given free credits, When inspected against the money system, Then they are never purchasable and live outside the money ledger [cross: REQ-006].
   90: 
   91: ## I. Transparency
   92: 
   93: - **AT-004.46 (P0)** — Given a Discovery surface, When the NGO views it, Then remaining daily credits and each turn's cost are visible; and every negative balance delta corresponds exactly to a visible turn-cost record or the documented daily reset, while zero-cost actions (file attach, regeneration, error retry) produce no delta — credits are never silently removed. [cx r2: made "never silently removed" a checkable delta invariant]

    1: # AI4DEV-3 — AT harness + at-config registry (working spec)
    2: 
    3: **Board:** AI4GOOD-DEV · project W0 Bring-up · vendor-native (closes on merge, no /pm-done gate).
    4: **Role:** the keystone of the verify layer. Every `bun run at:verify` in the skills — the inner
    5: loop's `--tier loop`, `/dev-end`'s assumed-verify, `/pm-done`'s gate — resolves to machinery
    6: that does not exist until this ships. Until then `/pm-done` fails closed by design.
    7: 
    8: This item bundles two things: the **engine that runs acceptance tests**, and the **registry of
    9: every configured number** those tests read.
   10: 
   11: ## Done criteria (functional — this is infrastructure, not itself AT-covered)
   12: 
   13: 1. `bun run at:verify req-0NN --tier <loop|integration|drill>` runs any requirement's suite and
   14:    reports pass/fail PER AT id.
   15: 2. The AT↔code bijection checker passes: every P0 AT id of a requirement has exactly one
   16:    executable test tagged with it; no executable test claims an id that is not in the suite.
   17: 3. The at-config registry is the single source for every configured number; no test hard-codes
   18:    a pinned value.
   19: 4. Proven on one real suite translated end-to-end (proving ground — see scope boundary).
   20: 5. One authored test body demonstrably runs on BOTH surfaces — fixtures and a real screen — from
   21:    a single source. The wiring leaves' `--wired` pass is a RE-RUN of already-assigned ids; if a
   22:    test has to be rewritten to drive a screen, the manifests' "no new AT ids" contract is broken
   23:    and the bijection silently becomes a claim about two different tests sharing a number. No
   24:    product screen exists yet, so prove the seam against the design session's static screens
   25:    (`design/screens/`).
   26: 
   27: ## Part A — the harness (runner + the capabilities the tests assume)
   28: 
   29: 1. **Runner + command surface.** `bun run at:verify req-0NN --tier <tier>`: resolve the executable
   30:    tests for a requirement by AT id, build a clean world, run, tear down, report per id. The
   31:    command shape is fixed — all 30 manifests' done contracts cite it verbatim.
   32: 2. **Tiers — one test, three depths.** `loop` (fast, more stubbed — the inner cycle),
   33:    `integration` (real components + real test DB — the /pm-done gate), `drill` (heaviest —
   34:    fault injection, adversarial). Lets one AT statement serve both the coding loop and the hard
   35:    gate without a rewrite.
   36: 3. **Fixture harness — the "Given" worlds.** Build/tear-down of product-real starting states
   37:    (an NGO; projects across the 9 lifecycle states; ledger rows; blockers; thread messages)
   38:    behind one seam. Reuses/extends the design session's fixture pack. Isolation between tests.
   39: 4. **Controlled clock.** Advance time on demand — funding's 7-day expiry, the UTC daily credit
   40:    reset, blocker aging 48h/7d, abandonment 14d/21d, short-lived link expiry, year-scale
   41:    no-decay. Many tests say "controlled clock" explicitly.
   42: 5. **Sentinels — plant/scan, presence AND absence.** Fact-in-a-file cited in scope (AT-004.16;
   43:    AT-032.12 across all 7 formats); body sentinel that must appear in NO store (AT-009.26);
   44:    key-shaped push that must be BLOCKED (AT-009.29); deleted→recovered file returning its
   45:    original content (AT-032.11).
   46: 6. **Fault injection.** Force a failure at a chosen point: notification atomicity under an
   47:    induced fault between transition and event write (AT-016.09); tamper-evident credit events
   48:    (AT-014.04). Assert rollback / atomicity.
   49: 7. **Semantic oracles.** Judge the MEANING of AI output where string-match cannot: rejection
   50:    copy instructs-never-accuses (AT-009.07); Opus yields a valid scope over 5–10 turns
   51:    (AT-004.10); the assistant's four framed answers (AT-033.07). A judge model or rubric.
   52: 8. **Provider/vendor simulation at the seams.** Controllable stand-ins the tests drive.
   53:    AMENDED (founder ruling, 2026-08-04, on AI4DEV-21's Gate 1 finding): the
   54:    email/notification provider stand-in ships with the H5 item (it has a consuming test,
   55:    AT-016.11); the five named vendor stand-ins — Anthropic per-workspace usage/cost report
   56:    (the whole money path — provider-truth conformance, 20/5/0% stop, nightly
   57:    reconciliation), Stripe (checkout/webhooks/disputes), GitHub, Lovable credit status,
   58:    Linear — are each built with the FIRST test suite that consumes them, never ahead of a
   59:    consumer, because a sim contract authored without its consuming test is a guess that gets
   60:    rewritten when the real suite arrives. Durable owners: board items AI4DEV-38 (Anthropic
   61:    usage/cost sim), AI4DEV-39 (Stripe sim), AI4DEV-40 (GitHub sim), AI4DEV-41 (Lovable
   62:    credit sim), AI4DEV-42 (Linear tree sim), all under this parent so it cannot fold while
   63:    any is missing. Their contracts land in `tests/at/harness/contracts.ts` with conformance
   64:    tests when built (rule 5 below).
   65: 9. **AT↔code bijection checker.** The code-level sibling of `loop/decomp/check-tree.ps1`:
   66:    every P0 id has a tagged executable test, no invented ids. Prevents silent skips.
   67: 10. **Per-id reporting** consumable by the skills (green/red per AT id + summary).
   68: 
   69: ## Part B — the at-config registry (single source of pinned numbers)
   70: 
   71: One place holding every configured value; tests read it, never hard-code. Contents (initial):
   72: gateway latency p95 ≤300ms + first-chunk ≤500ms (PROVISIONAL — pending founder SLO); AUP
   73: residual removal ≤15min; monitor propagation ≤5min; $50 funding minimum; $200 first-fund cap +
   74: per-day cap; tier grants 10/30; blocker aging 48h/7d; abandonment 14d/21d; 4-hour restore
   75: objective; file per-file + per-project size caps; authorized-link TTL; OD-4 binding-check
   76: threshold; OD-7 PRD-gate threshold. One edit re-tunes every test that uses a value — the reason
   77: harness + registry ship together.
   78: 
   79: ## Three execution surfaces (matches the manifests' surface tags)
   80: 
   81: - **backend** — against edge functions + the test DB.
   82: - **ui** — the wired re-runs, driving real screens. NOTE: the design session PLANNED a
   83:   design-check Playwright harness at `tests/design/` but never built it — only the static
   84:   screens, change orders, gate reports and log exist. There is no UI test foundation to inherit;
   85:   this item builds it. (The design checks themselves — never-show list, units discipline — are a
   86:   standing suite over the screen set, NOT per-requirement AT work; out of scope here.)
   87: - **skill** — REQ-028's tests in a harness-driven Claude Code session against fixture repos.
   88: 
   89: **Surface is an axis independent of tier.** A test is authored once and selected by id; the
   90: harness decides which surface it drives. 22 of 30 requirements are `mixed` and 21 carry a wiring
   91: leaf, so this is the common case, not an edge case.
   92: 
   93: ## Decisions that must land BEFORE the first suite is translated
   94: 
   95: These three are conventions, not code — none of them needs the harness to exist. All three are
   96: cheap now and expensive later: every suite translated before they are settled has to be rewritten.
   97: 
   98: 1. **The surface seam + the authoring rule.** A test that will be re-run through a screen must
   99:    never poke a fixture directly; it asserts through a seam the harness points at either the
  100:    fixture world or a rendered screen. Write the rule down where test authors will meet it
  101:    (`/dev-start`'s brief), because the just-in-time authoring moment for a UI-relevant test is
  102:    its OWNING leaf — early — while the wired re-run happens at the wiring leaf, late. An author
  103:    who does not anticipate the second run produces a test that cannot have one. RESOLVED
  104:    (founder, 2026-07-28): tests interact with the system ONLY through a driver interface
  105:    supplied by the harness; the harness provides a fixture driver now and a screen driver
  106:    later; a test that imports or touches fixture internals directly is invalid.
  107: 2. **A stable-identifier convention for screen elements.** Lovable owns the interface and
  108:    regenerates markup on every message; a wired test pinned to whatever markup existed that day
  109:    breaks on the next Lovable turn. Nothing in `design/ui-ux-instructions.md` mandates test
  110:    handles today. Agree the convention and push it into Lovable's project knowledge (the channel
  111:    already carrying the governance rules) so it re-applies to every generation. DRAFTED:
  112:    `loop/bringup/testid-convention-draft.md` — awaiting founder ratification.
  113: 3. **Where the ui tag lives.** 21 manifests scope `--wired` to "the ui-tagged subset"; no
  114:    acceptance file carries such a tag — the subset is named in 21 places and defined in zero.
  115:    Decide: marked per test in `acceptance/at-req-0NN.md`, or declared per leaf in the manifest.
  116:    Until this lands, `--wired` has no defined input. RESOLVED (founder, 2026-07-28): the ui tag
  117:    lives in the executable test registration — `atTest` accepts a surface marker; the acceptance
  118:    markdown files are NOT edited; `--wired` selects registered ui-marked ids and fails the run if
  119:    a wiring leaf's selection is empty.
  120: 
  121: ## Suite-authoring rules (founder-ratified 2026-07-29, after the REQ-016 audit)
  122: 
  123: Origin: the founder suspected the translated REQ-016 suite was overbuilt; an audit plus one
  124: codex round (gpt-5.6-sol, xhigh) confirmed the WASTE IS IN THE TRANSLATION PATTERN, NOT THE
  125: PLAN — the ~658 P0 ids stay; the acceptance docs are NOT reopened. These rules bind every suite
  126: translated from here on. The REQ-016 suite predates them and is retrofitted when H2 lands.
  127: Verdict trail: two of the auditor's own cuts were REFUTED and are recorded here as
  128: must-keeps, so nobody re-proposes them.
  129: 
  130: 1. **Capture once, assert many.** For each event execution, capture raw events, deliveries and
  131:    linked outcomes under the returned eventId, then FREEZE that snapshot (immutable — one lens
  132:    that normalizes or de-duplicates in place hides evidence from the next). Every P0 id whose
  133:    assertions only PROJECT that evidence (recipients, channels, payload, body, ops outcomes)
  134:    consumes the snapshot without re-firing. In REQ-016 this makes ~11 integration-tier firings
  135:    free: AT-016.04/.05/.12 become lenses over AT-016.03's capture.
  136: 2. **Generic self-checks live in the harness, once — with their own conformance tests.** Sentinel
  137:    value quality (length/uniqueness), rejection of unknown fault points, failure on clearing a
  138:    never-fired fault, restart epoch change, controlled-clock product wiring: implemented and
  139:    independently tested in the harness, never re-asserted per suite. HARD CAVEAT (codex): a bug
  140:    in a centralized guard green-lights all 30 suites at once — e.g. a fault handle that counts
  141:    "armed" as "triggered" makes every atomicity test pass on nothing. The conformance tests are
  142:    therefore not optional polish; they are the load-bearing wall. Domain-SPECIFIC controls stay
  143:    in the suites: no-fault control runs, mid-flight-not-yet-delivered checks, meaningful-config
  144:    guards, role-actually-moved checks — those are scenario evidence, not boilerplate.
  145: 3. **Fresh world only when state demands it.** A new open() only when the scenario changes
  146:    fault/provider/clock/process/role state, or when zero work could satisfy the assertion (then
  147:    include a positive control). Register each P0 id exactly once; loop its full oracle matrix
  148:    INSIDE the test — never sample, never mint per-row tests.
  149: 4. **Must-keeps (cuts proposed and REFUTED — do not re-propose):** (a) the sole-writer proof
  150:    keeps its domain firings — the provider-side orphan check is vacuous when nothing fires, and
  151:    a static import scan cannot see a sender using a raw client; the firings ARE the evidence.
  152:    (b) Oracle logic (channel rules, payload predicates, pair counting) and harness primitives
  153:    get focused unit tests of their own — they are code nothing else checks, and per rule 2 a
  154:    silent oracle bug would invalidate every suite. The tiers do not substitute for this.
  155: 5. **Shared contract, thin adapters.** Tier/clock/sentinel/fault/config/vendor contracts come
  156:    from ONE shared harness package (with the conformance tests above); a suite defines locally
  157:    only its requirement-specific SUT and fixture adapter. `_contract.ts` in REQ-016 was the
  158:    pre-harness stopgap, not the pattern.
  159: 
  160: ## Dependencies
  161: 
  162: - Integration tier needs a real test database → the **staging Supabase item (AI4DEV-6)** is a
  163:   sibling it leans on.
  164: - No downstream can be verified until this exists; it is intentionally first in W0.
  165: 
  166: ## Scope boundary — what this does NOT include
  167: 
  168: It builds the ENGINE + capabilities, not the ~658 per-requirement tests. Those are translated
  169: **just-in-time, test-first, when each requirement is pulled** (`/dev-start`'s brief points at
  170: the leaf's verify set). This item ships **one real suite translated end-to-end** as its proving
  171: ground — **REQ-016 (Notifications)** recommended: 12 P0, small, and it exercises the emitter,
  172: atomicity/fault-injection, and provider-acceptance paths in one suite.
  173: 
  174: ## Suggested internal breakdown (for when this item is pulled)
  175: 
  176: - **H0 Conventions** — the three decisions above, settled and written down. No code; blocks H7
  177:   and every just-in-time translation that follows.
  178: - **H1 Runner + tiers + surfaces + reporting** — the `at:verify` command, tier dispatch, the
  179:   surface seam and `--wired` selection, per-id report, the bijection checker.
  180: - **H2 Fixture harness + controlled clock** — world build/teardown seam + time control.
  181: - **H3 Sentinels + fault injection** — plant/scan utilities + induced-fault hooks.
  182: - **H4 Semantic-oracle harness** — the judge/rubric surface.
  183: - **H5 Vendor sims** — the email/notification provider stand-in now; Anthropic usage/cost,
  184:   Stripe, GitHub, Lovable, Linear stand-ins deferred to their first consuming suites
  185:   (AI4DEV-38..42 — see Part A item 8's amendment, founder 2026-08-04).
  186: - **H6 at-config registry** — the pinned-value module every test reads.
  187: - **H7 Proving ground** — translate REQ-016's 12 P0 end-to-end at integration tier; green. Its
  188:   in-app surface also exercises done-criterion 5 (one authored body, both surfaces).
  189: 
  190: ## Open questions for the build
  191: 
  192: - Test framework (Vitest/Playwright split for backend vs ui) — decide at H1. Constrained by the
  193:   surface seam: whatever is chosen, one authored test must reach both surfaces.
  194: - Semantic-oracle model + determinism strategy (fixed seed / rubric thresholds) — H4.
  195: - How much the `loop` tier stubs vs `integration` — the stub boundary — H1/H2.
  196: - Provisional pins (gateway p95, OD-4, OD-7) stay flagged in the registry until founder-set.

    1: # AT-REQ-009 — LLM Gateway: Virtual Keys, Caps & Inline Fuel Metering
    2: 
    3: Source: requirements/req-009.md (prd-mvp.md REQ-009). Dependencies: REQ-006, REQ-024, REQ-034, REQ-012/027/007 (key-termination hooks). Only provider surface: the model API through the gateway.
    4: 
    5: **Boundary note:** REQ-009 owns the gateway (auth, binding, injection, proxying, privacy), key lifecycle, the provider-workspace money path, and the fuel-threshold monitor. Money bookkeeping is owned by REQ-006; the attribution log's schema and writers by REQ-034; repo-side seeding of the marker and env hygiene by REQ-008; deactivation triggers by REQ-007/012/027 — `[cross:]` here.
    6: 
    7: ## A. Keys & authentication
    8: 
    9: - **AT-009.01 (P0)** — Given kickoff fires, When keys are minted, Then exactly one workspace-scoped virtual key exists for the (volunteer, project) pair, its value is retrievable exactly once (a second retrieval is impossible), and a sentinel scan of all platform logs finds no key material.
   10: - **AT-009.02 (P0)** — Given a standard unmodified Claude Code session configured with only the gateway base URL and the virtual key, When the volunteer runs a request, Then it completes — standard Claude Code works unchanged.
   11: - **AT-009.03 (P0)** — Given a sentinel provider credential, When every platform-controlled surface is scanned — gateway responses (headers + body), platform logs, traces, dashboard diagnostics, client-visible configuration, and persistent stores — Then the sentinel appears nowhere outside the authorized gateway secret store — the real credential is never exposed on any surface. [cx r2: widened from response-only to all platform surfaces]
   12: - **AT-009.04 (P0)** — Given three keys — revoked, never-existed, and malformed — When each is used, Then each rejection is exactly HTTP 401, the rejections are externally identical (same status, same body shape, no revoked-vs-nonexistent distinction), and a provider-side capture shows ZERO upstream calls for all three — invalid keys are rejected before forwarding. [cx r2: exact 401 + no-upstream-call assertions added]
   13: - **AT-009.05 (P0)** — Given a project at 0% fuel, When a request is made with a valid key, Then the proxied rejection states its cause (fuel exhaustion) — the one exception to flat rejections.
   14: - **AT-009.06 (P0)** — Given an active key and a revoked key, When the authenticated dashboard is read, Then it shows each existing key's status detail that the external surface withholds — diagnostics live only on the dashboard. [cx: narrowed — no per-key records exist for never-existed/malformed credentials]
   15: - **AT-009.07 (P0)** — Given a rejected request and a failed setup, When the messaging renders, Then it contains remediation instructions and no accusatory language — instructs, never accuses (semantic oracle over the copy).
   16: 
   17: ## B. Project binding & injected governance
   18: 
   19: - **AT-009.08 (P0)** — Given a session whose committed project-binding marker matches the key's project, When a substantive request is made, Then it passes; Given the marker names a different project, Then the request is rejected with an instructive message naming the KEY's project.
   20: - **AT-009.09 (P0)** — Given the substantive-request threshold configuration (OD-4), When a below-threshold request is made without a valid marker, Then it reaches the provider (upstream capture shows the call); When an at/above-threshold request is made without a valid marker, Then it receives the defined instructive rejection and produces NO upstream call — threshold enforcement observable at the provider boundary, not claimed internally. [cx r2: both branches given observable outcomes]
   21: - **AT-009.10 (P0)** — Given two consecutive forwarded requests (provider-side capture fixture), When their payloads are inspected, Then each carries the injected project-scope rule (decline/redirect unrelated requests) and the never-change-Linear-status rule — injected on every request, not once per session. [cross: REQ-026]
   22: - **AT-009.11 (P0)** — Given a session whose repo-side files are altered (marker deleted, third-party settings changed), When requests flow, Then governance injection and provider limits still apply unchanged — enforcement lives only on the gateway and provider surfaces; repo files are never trusted to enforce; only the binding tripwire reacts to the marker.

   24: ## C. Money path & fuel thresholds
   25: 
   26: > **Resolved [founder, d69]:** verified provider facts killed the spend-limit-as-ceiling model (limits are Console-only and monthly-reset; keys are Console-minted but API-flippable). The ruled design: fuel splits as provider budget = gross ÷ (1 + share rate); the platform monitor prices the 1-minute usage report at official rates (provisional) and flips the workspace key INACTIVE at the provider when remaining ≤ the stopping distance; re-arm above a higher line (hysteresis); ledger conforms nightly to billed cost; low per-workspace rate limits (velocity) + coarse monthly spend fuse (catastrophe) are set once at reserve creation. This also resolves the ÷1.15 share arithmetic (tension #4).
   27: 
   28: - **AT-009.12 (P0)** — Given a project funded $115 with a 15% share rate, When its provisioning is read, Then the bound reserve workspace carries its pre-set low rate limits and coarse spend fuse (untouched by funding), and the monitor's provider budget for the project is exactly $100 (gross ÷ 1.15); after a $34.50 top-up, Then the budget is exactly $130 — no provider limit changes on any funding event. [d69: rewritten — the spend limit no longer tracks fuel]
   29: - **AT-009.13 (P0)** — Given a project whose remaining provider budget reaches the stopping distance (controlled spend fixture), When the monitor acts, Then the workspace key is set INACTIVE at the provider, the provider rejects the next request, and the gateway proxies that rejection stating the cause; When a top-up lifts remaining above the re-arm line, Then the key is set active and the next request passes with no human step; a top-up that lifts remaining above the stop line but BELOW the re-arm line does NOT re-arm — hysteresis prevents flapping. [d69: rewritten — key-status stop replaces the spend-limit wall]
   30: - **AT-009.36 (P0)** — Given a project stopped for a ranked cause (chargeback, AUP, NGO revoke-now, or archival — four fixtures), When a top-up lands, Then the key stays inactive in every case — a top-up clears only the `exhausted` stop. [d69: added — stop-cause precedence]
   31: - **AT-009.37 (P0)** — Given the provider usage report made stale (sentinel: no fresh buckets past the freshness deadline) while the gateway still observes traffic, OR a key-flip that never confirms, When the watchdog evaluates, Then the project's virtual keys are cut and the admin is paged — fail closed, as a credential-status action with no money computation in the request path. [d69: added — watchdog]
   32: - **AT-009.38 (P0)** — Given a project spanning a calendar-month boundary (controlled clock), When the month rolls, Then the platform's remaining-budget figure carries over unchanged (cumulative, never reset) and late prior-month usage buckets still debit the project — only the provider's coarse fuse resets. [d69: added — month-boundary]
   33: > **Resolved [founder, d67]:** the blocking boundary is exactly 0%, and "blocking" is a STATUS on the project surface — the blocker mirrors the provider's stop, it never enforces anything. Enforcement = provider spend limit only; gateway = proxy only. REQ-009's "nears zero" reworded to "at 0% the blocker's state flips to blocking". AT-009.13/14 stand as written.
   34: 
   35: - **AT-009.14 (P0)** — Given sentinel spend fixtures crossing the thresholds, When the platform monitor reads the provider's Admin/usage API, Then at 20% remaining the NGO receives a warning blocker, at 5% the volunteer is warned, and at 0% the blocker is blocking — all driven from the provider read, not gateway math. [cx: blocking boundary pinned to 0% per REQ-024, pending ruling] [cross: REQ-016/024]
   36: - **AT-009.15 (P0)** — Given the project's money records after the nightly conformance, When the spend-of-record is compared to the provider's billed cost for its workspace, Then they are equal — the LEDGER carries only billed cost; the intraday usage-priced figure is provisional (drives gauge and stop only) and never lands as a ledger money value. [d69: provisional-vs-final split made explicit] [cross: REQ-006]
   37: - **AT-009.16 (P0)** — Given the per-request usage rows the gateway captures, When their schema and consumers are inspected, Then the rows carry no money fields, feed only attribution (REQ-034), and no gate or ledger reads them. [cx r2: prd-mvp Out-of-Scope item 21 still lists "cost" in per-request metadata — a stale leftover contradicting the d64-r money-never-derived rule; PRD scrub flagged, test unchanged (token-only is the ruled invariant)] [cross: REQ-034]
   38: - **AT-009.17 (P0)** — Given the gateway's configuration surface AND a project with sentinel-high accumulated usage whose key is still active, When the config is inspected and the next request is made, Then no custom usage-cap setting exists and the request is still forwarded — no gateway decision path consults a money counter; velocity is provider-native (workspace rate limits) and the budget decision lives only in the out-of-band monitor. [cx: behavioral half] [d69: wording aligned — budget decision is the monitor's, enforcement is provider key status]
   39: - **AT-009.18 (P0)** — Given a request burst exceeding the workspace's provider rate limit (fixture), When the provider rejects, Then the gateway proxies the rate-limit rejection to the caller unchanged in meaning.
   40: 
   41: ## D. Streaming & interactivity
   42: 
   43: - **AT-009.19 (P0)** — Given a streaming request through the gateway, When the response is consumed, Then chunks arrive incrementally (more than one chunk, first chunk before generation completes) — streaming is preserved end to end, not buffered.
   44: - **AT-009.20 (P0)** — Given ≥30 paired requests (identical payloads, gateway vs direct-to-provider) in the test environment, When p95 timings are compared, Then the gateway's added overhead is ≤300ms per request and the first streamed chunk's added delay is ≤500ms.    10: - **AT-004.02 (P0)** — Given free-credit turns with controlled provider costs, When each completes, Then the credit charged holds a constant cost-to-credit ratio (proportional to platform cost, under a stated rounding rule), and each turn's cost is shown to the NGO.    11: - **AT-004.03a (P0)** — Given an UNVERIFIED-tier NGO at 0 credits on an unfunded project, When it sends a message, Then it is blocked and shown exactly: get vetted (→30), fund fuel now, or wait for the next day. [cx: split by tier]
   12: - **AT-004.03b (P0)** — Given a VETTED NGO at 0 credits on an unfunded project, When it sends a message, Then it is blocked and shown only the applicable remedies: fund fuel now, or wait — never "get vetted". [cx: split by tier — REQ-002]
   13: - **AT-004.49 (P0)** — Given free-phase Discovery over a day, When turns run against the allowance, Then total free-phase provider spend never exceeds the remaining allowance and the balance never goes negative — without prescribing whether an underfunded turn is preflight-rejected or hard-capped.
